Clone the repository using the project's Git URL.\n2. Navigate to the project directory.\n3. Install dependencies: `npm install`.\n4. Install the Base44 CLI: `npm install -g base44@latest`.\n\nSee the [Base44 CLI docs](https://docs.base44.com/developers/references/cli/get-started/overview) if you want to run Base44 commands directly.\n\n## Run Locally\n\nRun the full local development environment from the project root:\n\n```bash\nbase44 dev\n```\n\n`base44 dev` starts the local Base44 development backend and, when this app is configured for it, also starts the frontend dev server for you. Use the frontend URL printed by the command.\n\nFor example, when the Base44 project config includes a `serveCommand`, `base44 dev` can launch the frontend too:\n\n```json5\n{\n  \"site\": {\n    \"serveCommand\": \"npm run dev\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\nIn a Base44 project this lives in `base44/config.jsonc`.\n\n## Run Only The Frontend\n\nIf you only want to work on the frontend against the hosted Base44 backend, run:\n\n```bash\nnpm run dev\n```\n\nOpen the local URL printed by Vite.\n\n## Use The Hosted Backend\n\nFor frontend-only development, create or update `.env.local` in the project root:\n\n```bash\nVITE_BASE44_APP_ID=your_app_id\nVITE_BASE44_APP_BASE_URL=https://your-app.base44.app\n```\n\n`VITE_BASE44_APP_ID` identifies the Base44 app.\n\n`VITE_BASE44_APP_BASE_URL` tells the Base44 Vite plugin where to send local `/api` requests. Point it at your deployed Base44 app URL when you want the local frontend to use the hosted backend.\n\nWhen you use `base44 dev`, the command injects the local Base44 values for you, so `.env.local` is mainly needed for frontend-only workflows.\n\n## RedAgent API Integration\n\nThis app is a front end for the RedAgent HTTP API (`serve_api.py` in the RedAgent\nproject). Start the API alongside the frontend:\n\n```bash\ncd /path/to/redAgent \u0026\u0026 python serve_api.py     # 0.0.0.0:8000\n```\n\n**Requests go to the same-origin path `/redagent`**, which the Vite dev server\nproxies to the API. This is deliberate: the API ships no CORS middleware, so the\nbrowser cannot call it cross-origin. Point the proxy elsewhere with:\n\n```bash\nVITE_REDAGENT_API_URL=http://10.0.0.5:8000 npm run dev\n```\n\nThe base URL can be overridden at runtime in **Settings → RedAgent API**, but an\nabsolute URL only works if you add CORS server-side or front the API with your\nown reverse proxy.\n\n\u003e **Deploying the built app:** `dist/` has no proxy. Serve `/redagent` from the\n\u003e same origin via your web server (nginx/Caddy `proxy_pass` to the API), or set\n\u003e an absolute base URL and enable CORS on the API.\n\n### What the integration covers\n\n| Endpoint | Used by |\n|---|---|\n| `GET /health` | Dashboard connectivity banner, Settings health check |\n| `GET /api/v1/modes` | New Scan — per-mode tool list and notes |\n| `POST /api/v1/scans` | New Scan submit |\n| `GET /api/v1/scans/{job_id}` | Scan tracking, history refresh |\n| `GET /api/v1/scans/{job_id}/report` | Report page |\n\nThings worth knowing about the API's shape, which the UI works around:\n\n- **Unknown request fields are rejected with `400`.** `src/lib/redagentApi.js`\n  owns the mapping to the exact accepted field set (note the British spelling:\n  `authorised_by`, `authorisation_ref`).\n- **White-box takes server-side filesystem paths** (`repos`). There is no upload\n  or clone endpoint — place the code on the API host first.\n- **There is no \"list scans\" endpoint**, and the job registry is in-memory, so it\n  is lost when the API restarts. History is kept per-browser in\n  `src/lib/scanStore.js` and refreshed from the API for in-flight jobs.\n- **Job status carries no progress percentage or target**, so the tracking page\n  shows elapsed time and an indeterminate bar rather than a fabricated number.\n- `GET .../report` returns `409` until the job finishes.\n\n**Demo Mode** (Settings) serves canned sample data in the real wire shapes so the\nUI can be explored with no backend. It is off by default and banners itself while\nactive — with it off, connection failures surface as errors instead of silently\nshowing fake results.\n\n## Publish Your Changes\n\nAfter pushing your changes to git, open the Base44 dashboard and publish the app:\n\n```bash\nbase44 dashboard open\n```\n\n## Docs \u0026 Support\n\nDocumentation: [https://docs.base44.com/Integrations/Using-GitHub](https://docs.base44.com/Integrations/Using-GitHub)\n\nBase44 CLI command reference: [https://docs.base44.com/developers/references/cli/commands/introduction](https://docs.base44.com/developers/references/cli/commands/introduction)\n\nSupport: [https://app.base44.com/support](https://app.base44.com/support)\n","ip":"127.0.0.1","timestamp":"2026-08-05T21:16:01.162083507+05:30","curl-command":"curl -X 'GET' -d '' -H 'Accept: */*' -H 'Accept-Language: en' -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0 (Debian; Linux x86_64) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/132.0.0.0 Safari/537.36' 'http://localhost:5174/README.md'","matcher-status":true}
